### अभ्यास प्रश्न

1. What time of day is it at the beginning of the story?
   A. 9:00 in the morning
   B. 12:00 noon
   C. 6:00 in the evening
   D. 9:00 in the evening
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The story states "It was nine o'clock in the evening" at the very beginning.

2. How long had the house in Lucknow been vacant?
   A. 1 week
   B. 2 weeks
   C. 3 weeks
   D. 1 month
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions "After a month of vacancy, the house in Lucknow had been opened up".

3. Who informed the servants of the arrival of the women?
   A. A phone call
   B. A letter
   C. A telegram
   D. An email
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- It states the servants were "informed by one terse telegram of the arrival of the daughter of the house and the two women accompanying her".

4. What feature did the gardener suggest working on again?
   A. The old fountain
   B. The old gazebo
   C. The old swing set
   D. The old Mountain
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text says "the gardener had even suggested working the old Mountain again".

5. How many bedrooms were aired and prepared?
   A. Two
   B. Three
   C. Four
   D. Five
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The story mentions "four bedrooms aired and prepared".

6. What was the atmosphere of the house compared to?
   A. A hospital waiting room
   B. An expensive nursing-home for convalescents
   C. A funeral parlor
   D. A summer holiday resort
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states "Its atmosphere was more like that of an expensive nursing-home for convalescents."

7. What was the mother knitting?
   A. A scarf
   B. A sweater
   C. Baby garments
   D. A blanket
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- It mentions "She kept her fingers busy with baby garments that she was knitting".

8. Who was the mother knitting the garments for?
   A. Her own baby
   B. Her grandchild
   C. A small orphan admitted to her creche
   D. A neighbor's child
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The mother was knitting "for a small orphan who had just been admitted into her creche as a special case".

9. What kind of dog was Nila trying to befriend?
   A. A dachshund
   B. A Pomeranian
   C. A poodle
   D. A Labrador
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nila "sat trying to make friends with a dim-witted Pomeranian".

10. Where was the telegram expected to arrive from?
   A. England
   B. America
   C. Switzerland
   D. Australia
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- When discussing the telegram, the mother says "But from Switzerland it will naturally take some time."

11. How many people originally lived in the large house?
   A. Two
   B. Three
   C. Four
   D. Five
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nila asks "How did they manage to live here- just the two of them?"

12. What kind of décor did the house have?
   A. Minimalist modern
   B. French country
   C. Victorian hodge-podge
   D. Traditional Indian
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nila complains about "the things that are crammed into it - a Victorian hodge-podge."

13. Who did they find a letter from in the desk?
   A. Maya's father
   B. Maya's husband
   C. Maya's brother
   D. Maya's lawyer
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The mother mentions "There was a brother too. The one whose letter we found in the desk."

14. What was Maya doing upstairs?
   A. Crying
   B. Sleeping
   C. Opening cupboards and pulling out drawers
   D. Packing her belongings
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- They could hear Maya "delightedly opening cupboards, pulling out drawers".

15. How did Maya greet Nila and her mother when they arrived?
   A. With tears
   B. With anger
   C. With a feline embrace
   D. With indifference
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nila was "inflamed by the recollection of the girl greeting them with her feline embrace".

16. What did Maya insist on bringing Nila and her mother?
   A. Coffee and cookies
   B. Tea and sweets
   C. Lemonade and cake
   D. Juice and fruit
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya insisted on "bringing them tea, sweets" even as she recounted her horrific story.

17. What did Maya do to Gautama according to her own account?
   A. Slapped him
   B. Pushed him hard so he fell
   C. Locked him in a room
   D. Poisoned his food
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya recounts in a casual manner "So then I pushed him, hard, and he fell."

18. Where did Nila's mother say the fewer people that see and hear Maya's story the better?
   A. At the police station
   B. At the hospital
   C. In general
   D. At the funeral
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- When Nila worries about Maya's story getting out, her mother thoughtfully says "the less people that see her and hear her story, the better."

19. Where will the doctors have to be told Maya's story according to Nila?
   A. At the hospital
   B. At the courthouse
   C. At the asylum
   D. At the morgue
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nila says "the doctors will have to be told at the asylum when she is taken there."

20. What had the doctor already done for Maya?
   A. Prescribed her medication
   B. Diagnosed her illness
   C. Scheduled her surgery
   D. Arranged her funeral
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The mother states matter-of-factly "The doctor has already diagnosed her illness."

21. Who did Maya say had to live between her and Gautama?
   A. Gautama
   B. Herself
   C. Both of them
   D. Neither of them
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Maya insists with calm certainty that "It had to be one of us, you see, and it was so clear that it was I who was meant to live."

22. What did Nila and her mother have great faith in?
   A. Religion
   B. Family
   C. Activity
   D. Fate
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- When confronted with difficult memories, it's mentioned "They both had great faith in activity."

23. What was Nila's mother doing to keep busy?
   A. Knitting
   B. Reading
   C. Cooking
   D. Cleaning
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- To stay occupied, the mother's "hands flew about her wool and knitting needles in a blur".

24. What did Nila and her mother avoid as they worked on winding wool?
   A. Eye contact
   B. Speaking
   C. Physical contact
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- As they wound wool, Nila and her mother sat "scrupulously avoiding contact".

25. What did Nila's mother compare their controlled thoughts to?
   A. A wild animal
   B. A natural disaster
   C. An explosion
   D. A disease
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Their controlled thoughts are described as "safe within her staff, would explode out of the bounds prescribed for them".

26. What shocking thing did Nila notice about her mother?
   A. She was smiling
   B. She was angry
   C. She was on the verge of crying
   D. She had fallen asleep
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nila is shocked to see "some new horrible, senile weakness in that mouth" indicating her mother was about to cry.

27. What broke the stillness of the dark garden and hush of the room?
   A. A scream
   B. Footsteps and laughter
   C. A gunshot
   D. Thunder
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The stillness was broken by "footsteps, uncertain but swift, skipping and running down towards them" and "a child's laughter".

28. Where were the footsteps and laughter coming from?
   A. The garden
   B. The kitchen
   C. The staircase
   D. The library
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The sounds seemed "very close, on the staircase perhaps."

29. What happened after the laughter stopped?
   A. There was silence
   B. There was a knock on the door
   C. They heard a different voice calling out in dread
   D. The power went out
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- After the laughter stopped, "they heard a different voice calling, shrilly and desperately, from some unimaginable realm of horror".

30. Who went to check on Maya after hearing her cries?
   A. Nila
   B. The mother
   C. A servant
   D. The doctor
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The mother took charge, saying "Someone must go to her" and "hurried out of the room" to go to Maya.

31. Which award did Anita Desai's novel "Cry, the Peacock" win?
   A. Booker Prize
   B. Pulitzer Prize
   C. Sahitya Academy Award
   D. None of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- "Cry, the Peacock" didn’t win any award, although she won Sahitya Academy Award for her novel Fire on the Mountain.

32. In which year was "Cry, the Peacock" published?
   A. 1960
   B. 1963
   C. 1965
   D. 1970
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that "Cry, the Peacock" was published in 1963.

33. What type of novel is "Cry, the Peacock"?
   A. Historical novel
   B. Science fiction novel
   C. Psychological novel
   D. Detective novel
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text describes "Cry, the Peacock" as a psychological novel.

34. Who is the heroine of the novel married to?
   A. Gautama
   B. Nirode
   C. Albino
   D. Ramsay
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the heroine Maya is married to Gautama, a lawyer who is twice her age.

35. What does Desai focus on in the novel?
   A. The loving relationship between Maya and Gautama
   B. The detached relationship between Maya and Gautama
   C. The friendship between Maya and Nila
   D. The rivalry between Maya and Gautama's mother
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Desai focuses on the detached relationship between Maya and Gautama.

36. What does the novel portray about Maya?
   A. A most sensitive woman suffering from neurotic fears and marital disharmony
   B. A happy and contented wife
   C. A successful career woman
   D. A rebellious teenager
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the novel portrays Maya, a most sensitive woman suffering from neurotic fears and marital disharmony.

37. How does Maya fail to identify herself?
   A. With her father's world
   B. With her husband Gautama's world
   C. With her own identity
   D. With the modern world
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya is a rebel woman who fails to identify herself with her husband Gautama's world.

38. What makes Maya feel insecure and powerless?
   A. Her lack of education
   B. Her total economic dependence on her husband
   C. Her poor health
   D. Her lack of friends
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's total economic dependence on her husband makes her feel rather insecure and powerless.

39. What is Maya in search of?
   A. A successful career
   B. A new husband
   C. A space in which she is at par with man
   D. A way to escape from her life
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya is in search of a new vista for a woman's world, a space in which she is at par with man.

40. What does the novel suggest about modern life?
   A. It is perfect and flawless
   B. It is doomed to disorderliness and destruction unless it considers and nourishes the tender feelings of women
   C. It is better than traditional life
   D. It has no impact on women's lives
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the novel clearly and evidently suggests that modern life is doomed to disorderliness and destruction unless it considers and nourishes the tender feelings of the women who form the nucleus in any society.

41. What do the writers of the post-Independence phase in India tend to do?
   A. Focus on historical events
   B. Move inward and try to get more psychologically intended
   C. Write about science and technology
   D. Ignore sociological effects on characters
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that in India, the writers of the post-Independence phase tend to move inward and try to get more and more psychologically intended.

42. Who have been able to see the Indian complexities from close quarters?
   A. Male writers
   B. Foreign writers
   C. Women novelists
   D. Children's authors
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that a number of women novelists have been able to see the Indian complexities from close quarters due to the peculiar situation of their existence.

43. What does fiction by women writers share?
   A. A minor segment of contemporary writing in Indian English literature
   B. A major segment of contemporary writing in Indian English literature
   C. An insignificant part of Indian English literature
   D. A declining trend in Indian English literature
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that fiction by women writers shares a major segment of the contemporary writing in Indian English literature.

44. What can one see through the eyes of women writers?
   A. A different world
   B. The same world as seen by male writers
   C. A world devoid of human achievement
   D. A world without dreams and desires
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that through the eyes of women writers, one can see a different world.

45. Who is Anita Desai considered to be among women novelists?
   A. A minor voice
   B. A controversial figure
   C. A leading voice
   D. A forgotten writer
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Anita Desai happens to be a leading voice among women novelists.

46. What does Desai tell in her novels?
   A. Simple stories about ordinary people
   B. Fascinating stories about individuals who have to traverse a ground too tricky and treacherous to handle smoothly
   C. Boring stories about predictable characters
   D. Unrealistic stories about perfect individuals
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Desai tells fascinating stories about individuals who have to traverse a ground too tricky and treacherous to handle smoothly.

47. What is Desai interested in analyzing and portraying?
   A. Historical events
   B. Scientific discoveries
   C. Human relationships
   D. Political ideologies
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Desai is interested in the analysis and portrayal of human relationships.

48. What is central to the main fictional concern in Desai's novels?
   A. Historical accuracy
   B. Political commentary
   C. Human relationships
   D. Scientific explanations
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that human relationships are not only of peripheral interest but also central to the main fictional concern in Desai's novels.

49. What does Desai's novel "Cry, the Peacock" explore?
   A. The search of a woman to fulfil herself basically as a human being, independent of her traditional roles
   B. The importance of women's traditional roles as daughter, wife, and mother
   C. The superiority of men over women
   D. The irrelevance of gender in modern society
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that "Cry, the Peacock" explores the search of a woman to fulfil herself basically as a human being, independent of her various traditional roles as daughter, wife, and mother.

50. Who determines Maya's fate in the novel?
   A. Maya herself
   B. The female characters in the novel
   C. The male society - her father, husband, and Albino, the astrologer
   D. The modern world
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's fate was determined by the male society - her father, husband, and Albino, the astrologer - who brought madness and death in her life.

51. What is Maya's only sin, according to the text?
   A. She is disobedient and rebellious
   B. She is lazy and unproductive
   C. She is sensitive, imaginative, passionate, and sensuous
   D. She is selfish and unkind
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya's only sin is that she is sensitive, imaginative, passionate, and sensuous.

52. Who does Maya represent in the novel?
   A. The content and satisfied woman
   B. The disturbed psyche of the modern Indian woman
   C. The ideal wife and mother
   D. The successful career woman
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya represents the disturbed psyche of the modern Indian woman.

53. What does the quest of the protagonist in "Cry, the Peacock" symbolize?
   A. The quest of one woman only
   B. The quest of all men in the society
   C. The quest of all women in the society towards understanding and acceptance of their predicament
   D. The quest for material wealth and success
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the quest of the protagonist in the novel is not only one woman's quest but the quest of all women in the society towards some understanding and acceptance of their predicament.

54. Who are the other women characters in the novel who symbolically present positivity and strength?
   A. Maya's mother and sister
   B. Gautama's sister and aunt
   C. Nila and Gautama's mother
   D. Maya's friends and neighbors
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that apart from Maya, there are other women in the novel, such as Nila and Gautama's mother, who symbolically present positivity and strength.

55. What does the beginning of the novel tell about the relationship between Maya and Gautama?
   A. Their loving and harmonious relationship
   B. The husband-wife alienation theme
   C. Their friendship and camaraderie
   D. Their professional rivalry
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that the very beginning of the novel tells the husband-wife alienation theme by unfolding the relationship of Maya and Gautama.

56. What was the result of Maya's marriage to Gautama?
   A. Maya found happiness and fulfilment
   B. Maya became a successful lawyer like her husband
   C. Maya became violent and killed her husband
   D. Maya and Gautama had many children
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that the marriage was never fruitful, and as a result, at the end, Maya became violent and killed her husband.

57. What did Maya suffer from due to her introversion?
   A. Physical illness
   B. Financial problems
   C. Utmost sexual dissatisfaction
   D. Professional failure
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya failed to establish effective communication with her husband as she was an introvert and suffered from utmost sexual dissatisfaction.

58. What is Maya's life shadowed by?
   A. The three-fold effect of love, happiness, and success
   B. The three-fold effect of death, doom, and destiny
   C. The three-fold effect of wealth, fame, and power
   D. The three-fold effect of education, career, and marriage
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Maya's life is shadowed by the three-fold effect of death, doom, and destiny.

59. What does Maya's act of murder represent?
   A. A random act of violence
   B. A sign of her love for her husband
   C. A revolt against the callous materialistic social order
   D. A way to gain financial independence
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text mentions that Maya's act of murder is a revolt against the callous materialistic social order.

60. What distinguishes Anita Desai from other Indian writers?
   A. Her focus on historical events
   B. Her involvement with Indian women and their reactions
   C. Her portrayal of perfect marriages
   D. Her avoidance of psychological themes
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The text states that Desai's involvement with Indian women and their reactions is different from others, which distinguishes her from other Indian writers.

61. What is a pidgin language?
   A. A language with a complex grammar system
   B. A language that develops between two or more groups that do not share a common language
   C. A language with a large vocabulary
   D. A language that is passed down from parents to children
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins develop as a simplified means of communication between groups without a shared language.

62. When did the history of pidgin languages begin?
   A. During the early days of global exploration and trade
   B. In the 20th century
   C. During the Industrial Revolution
   D. In the Middle Ages
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins emerged as European powers expanded their reach and encountered indigenous populations.

63. What is a key characteristic of pidgin languages?
   A. They have a complex grammatical structure
   B. They have a limited vocabulary
   C. They are used for a wide variety of purposes
   D. They have a large number of native speakers
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins often have a limited vocabulary focused on essential words for specific purposes.

64. Why do pidgin languages lack native speakers?
   A. They are not taught in schools
   B. They are learned as second languages by individuals who already speak another language natively
   C. They are too difficult to learn
   D. They are not recognized as official languages
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins are learned as second languages and not passed down from parents to children like native languages.

65. Which of the following is an example of a pidgin language?
   A. English
   B. Spanish
   C. Nigerian Pidgin English
   D. Mandarin Chinese
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nigerian Pidgin English developed during the colonial era and is widely spoken in Nigeria.

66. What is Hawaiian Pidgin English also known as?
   A. Hawaiian Creole English
   B. Tok Pisin
   C. Nigerian Pidgin English
   D. Bislama
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Hawaiian Pidgin English is also referred to as Hawaiian Creole English.

67. In what context do pidgin languages typically develop?
   A. In schools
   B. In situations of language contact
   C. In government institutions
   D. In religious settings
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins develop when groups with different native languages come into regular contact, often due to trade, colonialism, or migration.

68. What is the process of pidgin formation called?
   A. Creolization
   B. Linguistic adjustment
   C. Language shift
   D. Decreolization
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgin formation begins with linguistic adjustment, where speakers try to find ways to communicate using simplified language and borrowed words.

69. What role did trade and colonialism play in the development of pidgin languages?
   A. They hindered the development of pidgin languages
   B. They had no impact on the development of pidgin languages
   C. They played a significant role in the development of many pidgin languages
   D. They only influenced the development of pidgin languages in Africa
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins often developed to facilitate communication between colonizers and local populations in trading posts and colonies.

70. What is one of the most significant threats to pidgin languages?
   A. Decreolization
   B. Increasing number of native speakers
   C. Expansion of vocabulary
   D. Simplification of grammar
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Decreolization occurs when a creole language gradually loses its distinctive features and becomes more similar to the dominant language.

71. What is another threat to pidgin languages?
   A. Language contact
   B. Language shift
   C. Linguistic adjustment
   D. Creolization
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- As speakers become more proficient in the dominant language, they may gradually abandon the pidgin.

72. Why is it important to document and study pidgin languages?
   A. To understand the history and development of these languages
   B. To preserve the cultural heritage of the communities that speak them
   C. Both A and B
   D. Neither A nor B
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Documenting pidgin languages is crucial for understanding their history and preserving the cultural heritage of the communities that speak them.

73. What is the main purpose of a pidgin language?
   A. To serve as a language of education
   B. To facilitate basic communication between groups that do not share a common language
   C. To replace native languages
   D. To be used as an official language
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins develop as a simplified means of communication between groups without a shared language.

74. Which of the following is NOT a characteristic of pidgin languages?
   A. Simplified grammar
   B. Limited vocabulary
   C. Lack of native speakers
   D. Wide range of functions
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Unlike fully developed languages, pidgins are typically confined to specific domains and have a limited range of functions.

75. What is the vocabulary of a pidgin language typically limited to?
   A. Abstract concepts
   B. Words that are essential for the specific purposes for which the language is used
   C. Complex grammatical structures
   D. A wide range of topics
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgin vocabularies are usually focused on words essential for the specific purposes they are used for, such as trade or work-related interactions.

76. Which of the following is an example of a pidgin language that has become a national language?
   A. Hawaiian Pidgin English
   B. Nigerian Pidgin English
   C. Tok Pisin
   D. Bislama
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Tok Pisin, spoken in Papua New Guinea, has become one of the country's official languages.

77. In which century did Hawaiian Pidgin English emerge?
   A. 18th century
   B. 19th century
   C. 20th century
   D. 21st century
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Hawaiian Pidgin English developed on the Hawaiian plantations in the 19th century.

78. What is the process called when a creole language gradually loses its distinctive features and becomes more similar to the dominant language?
   A. Creolization
   B. Decreolization
   C. Language shift
   D. Linguistic adjustment
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Decreolization is the process in which a creole language gradually becomes more similar to the dominant language of the region.

79. What is essential for understanding the history and development of pidgin languages?
   A. Ignoring their cultural significance
   B. Focusing only on their vocabularies
   C. Studying their grammatical structures
   D. Recording and analyzing these languages
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Linguists and anthropologists are working to record and analyze pidgin languages to understand their history and development.

80. What insight do we gain by exploring the world of pidgin languages?
   A. Understanding the history and development of these languages
   B. Understanding the complex social, cultural, and economic factors that shape language use and evolution
   C. Both A and B
   D. Neither A nor B
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Exploring pidgin languages provides insight into their history, development, and the complex factors that shape language use and evolution.

81. What is the main reason for the lack of complex grammatical structures in pidgin languages?
   A. They are not taught in schools
   B. They are used for a limited range of functions
   C. They are simplified to facilitate basic communication
   D. They lack native speakers
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins rely on a streamlined set of rules to allow for basic communication, resulting in a lack of complex grammatical structures.

82. Which of the following is NOT a situation in which pidgin languages typically develop?
   A. Trade
   B. Colonialism
   C. Migration
   D. Formal education
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins typically develop in situations of language contact, such as trade, colonialism, or migration, but not in formal education settings.

83. What is the main difference between pidgin languages and fully developed languages?
   A. Pidgin languages have a complex grammar system
   B. Pidgin languages have a larger vocabulary
   C. Pidgin languages are used for a wide variety of purposes
   D. Pidgin languages have a limited range of functions
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Unlike fully developed languages, which can be used for a wide variety of purposes, pidgins are typically confined to specific domains.

84. What is the significance of pidgin languages lacking native speakers?
   A. It means they are not recognized as official languages
   B. It means they are not passed down from parents to children like native languages
   C. It means they are too difficult to learn
   D. It means they are not taught in schools
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Since pidgins are learned as second languages, they are not passed down from parents to children in the way that native languages are.

85. What is the importance of documenting and studying pidgin languages?
   A. To preserve the cultural heritage of the communities that speak them
   B. To understand the history and development of these languages
   C. To prevent decreolization and language shift
   D. Both A and B
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Documenting and studying pidgin languages is essential for both preserving the cultural heritage of the communities that speak them and understanding their history and development.

86. What is a creole language?
   A. A language that is a simplified version of another language
   B. A language that develops from a pidgin and becomes a stable, natural language
   C. A language that is only spoken by a small group of people
   D. A language that is not recognized as an official language
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Creoles are stable, natural languages that develop from pidgins.

87. What is the relationship between pidgin and creole languages?
   A. Pidgins and creoles are the same
   B. Pidgins always give rise to creoles
   C. Creoles always have a clear pidgin ancestor
   D. Pidgins provide the linguistic foundation upon which creoles develop
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Pidgins often serve as the linguistic foundation for creoles, but not all pidgins lead to creoles, and not all creoles have a clear pidgin ancestor.

88. What is one of the most notable features of creole languages compared to pidgins?
   A. Simplified grammar
   B. Limited vocabulary
   C. Expanded grammar and vocabulary
   D. Lack of native speakers
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Creoles have developed more complex grammatical structures and a richer lexicon compared to pidgins.

89. What is the process called when a creole language becomes the native language of a new generation of speakers?
   A. Pidginization
   B. Creolization
   C. Nativization
   D. Language shift
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nativization is the process in which a creole language becomes the native language of a new generation of speakers.

90. What is a key characteristic of creole languages in terms of native speakers?
   A. Creoles have no native speakers
   B. Creoles are learned as second languages
   C. Creoles are acquired as first languages by children in the community
   D. Creoles are only spoken by adults
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Unlike pidgins, creoles are acquired as first languages by children in the community.

91. What is the relationship between creole languages and identity?
   A. Creole languages are not associated with any particular identity or culture
   B. Creole languages are often an integral part of their speakers' cultural heritage and identity
   C. Creole languages are always associated with a single cultural group
   D. Creole languages are only used for business and trade
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- For many creole-speaking communities, the language is an integral part of their cultural heritage and a marker of their unique identity.

92. Which of the following is an example of a creole language?
   A. French
   B. Spanish
   C. Haitian Creole
   D. Mandarin Chinese
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Haitian Creole, also known as Kreyòl, is one of the most widely spoken creole languages in the world.

93. When did Haitian Creole emerge?
   A. In the early 16th century
   B. In the late 17th and early 18th centuries
   C. In the mid-19th century
   D. In the early 20th century
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Haitian Creole emerged in the late 17th and early 18th centuries on the French colony of Saint-Domingue (present-day Haiti).

94. What is Jamaican Patois also known as?
   A. Haitian Creole
   B. Gullah
   C. Jamaican Creole
   D. Tok Pisin
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Jamaican Patois is also referred to as Jamaican Creole.

95. Where is Gullah spoken?
   A. In Haiti
   B. In Jamaica
   C. In the Sea Islands of South Carolina and Georgia, United States
   D. In Papua New Guinea
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gullah is a creole language spoken in the Sea Islands of South Carolina and Georgia in the United States.

96. What is the process of nativization closely tied to in the development of creole languages?
   A. The spread of education
   B. The decline of slavery
   C. The process of a pidgin language becoming the native language of a new generation of speakers
   D. The standardization of the language
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The development of creole languages is closely tied to the process of nativization, where a pidgin becomes the native language of a new generation.

97. What role did slavery and plantations play in the development of many creole languages?
   A. They had no impact on the development of creole languages
   B. They hindered the development of creole languages
   C. They played a significant role in the development of many creole languages
   D. They only influenced the development of creole languages in the Caribbean
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- In many cases, creole languages emerged on plantations where enslaved Africans from various linguistic backgrounds were forced to work together.

98. What have been some of the challenges faced by creole languages in terms of their status and recognition?
   A. They have been widely recognized as official languages
   B. They have been seen as "real" languages by all scholars and policymakers
   C. There have been debates surrounding their legitimacy, with some questioning whether they are "real" languages
   D. They have not faced any challenges in terms of status and recognition
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Creole languages have often faced challenges, with some scholars and policymakers questioning whether they are "real" languages or merely "broken" versions of their lexifier languages.

99. What have linguists and anthropologists done to highlight the importance of creole languages?
   A. They have worked to document and analyze these languages
   B. They have ignored these languages in their research
   C. They have discouraged the use of creole languages
   D. They have only focused on the vocabularies of these languages
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Linguists and anthropologists have worked to document and analyze creole languages, highlighting their unique features and the complex factors that have shaped their development.

100. What efforts have been made to promote creole languages in Haiti?
   A. Haitian Creole has been banned from official use
   B. Haitian Creole has been recognized as an official language alongside French
   C. There have been no efforts to promote Haitian Creole
   D. Haitian Creole has been discouraged in educational settings
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- In Haiti, Haitian Creole has been recognized as an official language alongside French.

101. What do creole languages reflect about human language and culture?
   A. The uniformity of human language and culture
   B. The simplicity of human language and culture
   C. The resilience, creativity, and adaptability of human language and culture
   D. The limitations of human language and culture
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Creole languages are a testament to the resilience, creativity, and adaptability of human language and culture.

102. What can we gain by studying and celebrating creole languages?
   A. A deeper understanding of the linguistic features of these languages
   B. A deeper understanding of the rich tapestry of human diversity and cultural heritage that they represent
   C. Both A and B
   D. Neither A nor B
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Studying creole languages provides insights into both their unique linguistic features and the rich diversity of human culture and heritage.

103. What is an important step towards valuing and preserving the linguistic and cultural diversity of our world?
   A. The suppression of creole languages
   B. The recognition and promotion of creole languages
   C. The discouragement of multilingualism
   D. The adoption of a single global language
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The recognition and promotion of creole languages is an important step towards valuing and preserving linguistic and cultural diversity.

104. What is a notable difference between the grammar and vocabulary of pidgins and creoles?
   A. Pidgins have more complex grammar and vocabulary than creoles
   B. Creoles have more simplified grammar and vocabulary than pidgins
   C. Pidgins and creoles have the same level of grammatical and lexical complexity
   D. Creoles have more expanded grammar and vocabulary compared to pidgins
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- While pidgins have simplified grammar and limited vocabulary, creoles have developed more complex grammatical structures and a richer lexicon.

105. What is the importance of native speakers in the context of creole languages?
   A. Creole languages do not have native speakers
   B. The presence of native speakers is crucial to the development and stability of creole languages
   C. Native speakers hinder the development of creole languages
   D. Native speakers are not important for the stability of creole languages
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- The process of nativization, where creoles are acquired as first languages by children, is crucial to the development and stability of these languages.

106. How do creole languages often reflect the histories and cultural influences of their speakers?
   A. By excluding elements from various source languages and cultures
   B. By incorporating elements from a single source language and culture
   C. By incorporating elements from various source languages and cultures
   D. By not reflecting any historical or cultural influences
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Creole languages often incorporate elements from various source languages and cultures, reflecting the complex histories and cultural influences of their speakers.

107. What is the Gullah language an example of?
   A. A creole language that developed in the context of slavery and plantation life
   B. A creole language that developed in a context of free trade and commerce
   C. A pidgin language that never developed into a creole
   D. A creole language that has no connection to African American cultural heritage
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Gullah emerged as a result of contact between enslaved Africans and English-speaking colonizers on plantations in the 18th and 19th centuries.

108. What is the primary factor that leads to the nativization of a pidgin language?
   A. The pidgin being used in formal education
   B. The pidgin being recognized as an official language
   C. The pidgin being used extensively in a community and becoming the primary means of communication for a significant portion of the population
   D. The pidgin being taught to children in schools
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Nativization typically occurs when a pidgin is used extensively in a community and becomes the primary means of communication for a significant portion of the population.

109. What have been some of the debates surrounding the legitimacy of creole languages?
   A. Whether they are "real" languages or merely "broken" versions of their lexifier languages
   B. Whether they should be used in formal education
   C. Whether they have any cultural value
   D. Whether they should be standardized
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- There have been debates about whether creole languages are "real" languages or just "broken" versions of their lexifier languages.

110. What is a result of the growing recognition of the value and importance of creole languages?
   A. Efforts to discourage the use of creole languages
   B. Efforts to standardize and promote creole languages, particularly in education and official contexts
   C. Efforts to replace creole languages with more widely spoken languages
   D. Efforts to limit the influence of creole languages on other languages
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- There have been efforts to standardize and promote creole languages, especially in education and official contexts, as a result of growing recognition of their value and importance.

111. What does code mixing refer to?
   A. The practice of combining elements from two or more languages within a single utterance or conversation
   B. The practice of alternating between languages across utterances or conversational turns
   C. The practice of using only one language in a conversation
   D. The practice of translating words from one language to another
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Code mixing involves combining elements from two or more languages within a single utterance or conversation.

112. How is code mixing different from code switching?
   A. Code mixing and code switching are the same
   B. Code mixing involves alternating between languages across utterances or conversational turns
   C. Code switching involves combining elements from two or more languages within a single utterance or conversation
   D. Code mixing involves combining elements within a single utterance, while code switching involves alternating between languages across utterances
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Code mixing combines elements within a single utterance, while code switching alternates between languages across utterances.

113. What is intra-sentential code mixing?
   A. Mixing languages within a single word
   B. Mixing languages within a single sentence or clause
   C. Mixing languages across sentences or conversational turns
   D. Mixing languages only at the beginning of a sentence
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Intra-sentential code mixing involves mixing languages within a single sentence or clause.

114. What does intra-word code mixing involve?
   A. Combining elements from different languages within a single word
   B. Combining elements from different languages within a single sentence
   C. Combining elements from different languages across sentences
   D. Combining elements from different languages only at the end of a word
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Intra-word code mixing involves combining elements from different languages within a single word.

115. Which of the following is an example of code mixing?
   A. Speaking only in English
   B. Speaking only in Spanish

   D. Using English words or phrases within Spanish sentences

136. What does code switching involve?

137. How is code switching different from code mixing?

   D. Code switching only involves two languages, while code mixing can involve multiple languages
   **उत्तर:** C

138. What is inter-sentential code switching?

140. What is the purpose of tag switching?

169. How are grammatical structures handled in code switching?

170. What level of language proficiency is typically required for code switching?

   C. Climatic factors

176. How can code mixing and code switching serve as markers of identity?

178. What is the main difference between code mixing and code switching in terms of the level of language mixing?

179. How do code mixing and code switching differ in terms of grammatical integration?

180. How do code mixing and code switching differ in terms of consciousness?
   A. Code mixing is often conscious, while code switching is often unconscious
   B. Code mixing is often unconscious, while code switching is often conscious
   C. Both code mixing and code switching are always conscious
   D. Both code mixing and code switching are always unconscious
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Code mixing is often spontaneous and unconscious, while code switching tends to be a more conscious choice made by the speaker.

181. What is the difference between code mixing and code switching in terms of language proficiency?
   A. Code mixing requires higher proficiency in both languages than code switching
   B. Code switching requires higher proficiency in both languages than code mixing
   C. Code mixing and code switching require the same level of proficiency in both languages
   D. Language proficiency is not relevant for code mixing or code switching
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Code switching generally requires a higher level of proficiency in both languages compared to code mixing.

182. What is the importance of understanding code mixing and code switching?
   A. To appreciate the complexity and richness of multilingual communication
   B. To discourage the use of multiple languages
   C. To promote the use of a single language
   D. To simplify language use
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Understanding the characteristic features of code mixing and code switching is crucial for appreciating the complexity and richness of multilingual communication.

183. What is code mixing?
   A. The use of a single language in all contexts
   B. The alternation between languages across sentence boundaries
   C. The integration of elements from different languages within a single utterance
   D. The complete separation of languages in communication
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Code mixing involves the integration of elements from different languages within a single utterance or sentence.

184. What is code switching?
   A. The use of a single language in all situations
   B. The mixing of languages within a single word
   C. The alternation between languages within a single sentence
   D. The alternation between languages across sentence or clause boundaries
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Code switching refers to the alternation between two or more languages or language varieties across sentence or clause boundaries.

185. What is the main similarity between code mixing and code switching?
   A. They both involve the use of a single language
   B. They both occur in monolingual contexts
   C. They both serve important social, cultural, and communicative functions
   D. They both indicate a lack of language proficiency
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** Ex- Both code mixing and code switching serve important social, cultural, and communicative functions, allowing speakers to express their identities, navigate different contexts, and creatively combine their linguistic resources.

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

317. According to Meg, where did Stanley once give a concert?
   A. In a small club
   B. At a wedding
   C. In a big hall
   D. On the pier
   **उत्तर:** C

   B. A watch
   C. Champagne
   D. A standing ovation
   **उत्तर:** C

   C. He lost his sheet music

   D. It's his birthday
   **उत्तर:** D

   B. Take him out to dinner
   C. Have a party
   D. Buy him a present

   C. Her party dress

   B. Decorations

   B. Stanley's father

   C. Bring him out of himself

   C. A daisy

   **उत्तर:** B

   D. It's not mentioned
   **उत्तर:** B

   C. 9 o'clock
   D. 10 o'clock

   C. Lulu
   D. Meg

   D. All of the above

   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D

   C. How long are they staying?
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D

   B. Goldstein

   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

   D. All of the above

   B. A boy's drum
   C. A book
   D. A box of chocolates
   **उत्तर:** B

343. How does Stanley react to the birthday present?

   **उत्तर:** D

   D. Both B and C
   **उत्तर:** D

   D. Both B and C
   **उत्तर:** D

347. What does Stanley say about his past life and living in this house?

   C. He doesn't like living in someone else's house
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D

348. What does Stanley say about the lines on his face and his drinking?

   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D

   **उत्तर:** D

   **उत्तर:** D

   B. It's not a boarding house and never was
   C. It's a ridiculous house to pick
   D. Both B and C
   **उत्तर:** D

   D. All of the above

   D. That he has never heard the name Nat before

375. What does Stanley do after finally sitting down at Goldberg's insistence?

376. What does Goldberg ask Stanley about what he did the previous days?

377. What does McCann accuse Stanley of doing?

   B. Driving the old lady off her rocker
   C. Forcing the old man to play chess
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

378. What does Goldberg ask Stanley about taking fruit salts for his headache?

   D. She doubts it
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

570. How long has Stanley been living in Meg and Petey Boles's boarding house?
   A. A few months
   B. The past year
   C. Two years
   D. Five years
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

571. How is Stanley described in terms of his appearance and habits?
   A. Well-dressed and sociable
   B. Reclusive and unkempt
   C. Tidy and outgoing
   D. Fashionable and reserved
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

572. What does Stanley wear?
   A. A suit and tie
   B. Jeans and a t-shirt
   C. Filthy old pants and a pajama top
   D. A dress shirt and slacks
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

573. What would happen if Meg didn't make sure Stanley ate breakfast and drank his tea?
   A. He would starve
   B. He would never leave his bedroom
   C. He would go out for breakfast
   D. He would make his own meals
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

574. Why has Stanley come to this seaside town?
   A. To start a new life
   B. To hide from his past
   C. To enjoy the beach
   D. To find work
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

575. How does Stanley feel about leaving the boarding house?
   A. Excited to explore
   B. Indifferent
   C. Nervous but willing
   D. He feels he has nowhere to go
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

576. How does Stanley react to the arrival of Goldberg and McCann?
   A. He's happy to have company
   B. He's distraught
   C. He's indifferent
   D. He's curious about them
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

577. What effect do Goldberg and McCann have on Stanley?
   A. They make him feel guilty
   B. They make him feel appreciated
   C. They have no effect on him
   D. They make him feel nostalgic
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

578. How do Meg and Petey react to Stanley's distress caused by Goldberg and McCann?
   A. They try to protect him
   B. They confront Goldberg and McCann
   C. They hardly notice
   D. They encourage Goldberg and McCann
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

579. What shocking actions does Stanley take during the play?
   A. He tries to leave the boarding house
   B. He tries to strangle Meg and rape Lulu
   C. He confesses his past crimes
   D. He befriends Goldberg and McCann
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

580. What does Pinter portray Stanley as?
   A. A harmless victim
   B. Someone who has always been dangerous
   C. A reformed criminal
   D. A misunderstood hero
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

581. What is Stanley incapable of by the end of the play?
   A. Eating and drinking
   B. Sleeping
   C. Communicating and standing up for himself
   D. Walking and dressing himself
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

582. What does Meg lack that she tries to make up for in fastidiousness?
   A. Cleanliness
   B. Intelligence
   C. Kindness
   D. Patience
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

583. How does Meg treat Stanley?
   A. Strictly as a tenant
   B. As a close friend
   C. Both maternally and romantically
   D. With indifference
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

584. What does Meg care more about than acting in accordance with reality?
   A. Pleasing her guests
   B. Following routines
   C. Being flexible
   D. Adapting to change
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

585. How does Meg view Goldberg and McCann's intentions towards Stanley?
   A. She recognizes their malicious motives
   B. She thinks they want to help Stanley
   C. She's too naive to see their true intentions
   D. She doesn't care about their intentions
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

586. What does Meg focus on instead of the tension between Stanley, Goldberg, and McCann?
   A. Cleaning the house
   B. Throwing Stanley a birthday party
   C. Keeping Petey happy
   D. Preparing elaborate meals
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

587. How does Meg act the morning after Stanley's birthday party?
   A. Concerned about Stanley
   B. Angry at Goldberg and McCann
   C. As if nothing extraordinary has happened
   D. Regretful about the party
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

588. What does Petey tell Meg about Stanley at the end of the play?
   A. That he left with Goldberg and McCann
   B. That he's still sleeping upstairs
   C. That he's feeling better
   D. That he's planning to leave soon
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

589. How much time does Petey spend at the boarding house?
   A. Most of his time
   B. Equal time at home and work
   C. He's rarely at home
   D. He never leaves the house
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

590. Where does Petey work?
   A. At a factory
   B. At a restaurant
   C. At the nearby beach
   D. At a store
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

591. How does Petey handle Meg's obsession with order and routine?
   A. He tries to change her
   B. He argues with her
   C. He ignores her
   D. He indulges her
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

592. Who seems to be the only character truly concerned about Stanley's well-being?
   A. Meg
   B. Goldberg
   C. McCann
   D. Petey
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

593. What does Petey yell to Stanley as Goldberg and McCann take him away?
   A. "Don't forget to write!"
   B. "I'll come visit you soon!"
   C. "Don't let them tell you what to do!"
   D. "Good luck on your new adventure!"
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

594. What do Goldberg's multiple names (Nat, Simey, Benny) suggest about his past?
   A. That he's a man of many talents
   B. That he's had a straightforward life
   C. That his past is jumbled and inscrutable
   D. That he's a compulsive liar
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

595. What does Goldberg want most?
   A. To be wealthy
   B. To be feared
   C. To be full of wisdom
   D. To be famous
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

596. How does Goldberg win over Meg?
   A. By complimenting her cooking
   B. By offering to help with chores
   C. By complimenting her dress
   D. By giving her a gift
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

597. What is McCann's role in relation to Goldberg?
   A. He's Goldberg's boss
   B. He's Goldberg's equal partner
   C. He takes orders from Goldberg
   D. He and Goldberg have a rivalry
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

598. According to Goldberg, what was McCann's previous profession?
   A. A doctor
   B. A teacher
   C. A businessman
   D. A priest
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

599. What does Stanley's drum symbolize in the play?
   A. Stanley's desire for freedom
   B. Meg's fondness for order
   C. Goldberg's control over others
   D. McCann's hidden talents
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

600. What does Lulu encourage Stanley to do when she visits the boarding house?
   A. Play the piano
   B. Go for a walk with her
   C. Leave the boarding house permanently
   D. Stand up to Goldberg and McCann
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

601. How does Stanley respond to Lulu's invitation to go for a walk?
   A. He eagerly agrees
   B. He says he'll go later
   C. He declines, saying he can't at the moment
   D. He doesn't give a clear answer
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

602. What happens during the game of "blind man's buff" at Stanley's birthday party?
   A. Stanley has a pleasant time
   B. Stanley argues with Goldberg and McCann
   C. The game is played without incident
   D. Stanley attempts to rape Lulu
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

603. Who stops Stanley from attacking Lulu during the party?
   A. Meg and Petey
   B. Goldberg and McCann
   C. Lulu defends herself
   D. Nobody, the attack is successful
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

604. What does Lulu accuse Goldberg of the morning after the party?
   A. Stealing from her
   B. Lying about his identity
   C. Having sex with her without intending to start a relationship
   D. Plotting to harm Stanley
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

605. How does Goldberg respond to Lulu's accusation?
   A. He apologizes sincerely
   B. He denies the accusation
   C. He says she's now "a jump ahead"
   D. He becomes angry and defensive
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

606. What does McCann try to get Lulu to do after her conversation with Goldberg?
   A. Leave the boarding house
   B. Help them with Stanley
   C. Confess her sins
   D. Forget about the previous night
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

607. What is Lulu's response to McCann's demand?
   A. She confesses everything
   B. She argues with him
   C. She runs away
   D. She asks for Goldberg's help
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

608. Who is Monty?
   A. A doctor Goldberg and McCann take Stanley to see
   B. Meg and Petey's son
   C. A friend of Stanley's
   D. A character who never appears in the play
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

609. How does Goldberg speak about Monty to Petey?
   A. In a straightforward, honest way
   B. In a lighthearted, joking manner
   C. In an ominous and purposefully vague way
   D. He doesn't mention Monty to Petey at all
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।
